# I-harness 七源後端機制矩陣（2026-09-11）

> 基準：I-harness `m62` @ `48652c9c` · 方式：機械模組清單 ＋ 逐源並行子代理機制盤點 ＋ 跨源對帳 ＋ 對抗式引註驗證

本文件是**機制層**的七源對比。姊妹文件 `docs/audit/2026-09-11-sevenway-command-matrix.md`（D2）答的是**命令層**——哪個 harness 有 `/compact`、怎麼實作、我們該怎麼辦。本文件答的是**後端設計本身**：agent 迴圈長什麼形狀、子代理協定怎麼保證耐久、沙箱到底圍堵了什麼、壓縮是改寫還是投影。**兩者互補，不重複。**

## 為什麼列是「機制」而不是「模組」

七源的模組數從 **36 到 267**，而且**不可比**：

| 源 | 模組數 | 說明 |
|---|---|---|
| dsh | **267** | `packages/<area>/<package>`，大量是 Cordis 管線 |
| codex | **102** | 含 `ansi-escape`、`async-utils`、`build-info` 這類基建 |
| opencode-fork | 99 | 引擎在 `packages/core/src/*` |
| opencode | 97 | 同上 |
| grok | 82 | 含整個渲染器（`xai-ratatui-*`） |
| IH | 69 | 65 包 ＋ 2 app |
| cc-custom | 36 | Claude Code 系 |

模組對模組的表會**同時太吵又誤導**：`ansi-escape` 有沒有，跟一個 harness 能不能做事無關。所以**列是能力**——「四級輸入准入」「耐久任務協定與 recovery-required 分類」「啟動即套用的 OS 沙箱，寧可拒絕啟動也不裸跑」。

模組清單仍然存在，但它的角色是**讓「什麼都盤到了」變成可檢查的主張**：Phase 0 機械列出每個源的每一個模組，每個源的盤點代理必須**逐一交代**每個模組貢獻哪些機制、或為什麼不貢獻能力。組裝時這是一道**硬閘**——有任何模組沒被交代就拒絕產出文件並點名。

## 七源身份表（版本為本次實測，非目錄名）

| # | 源 | 路徑 | 語言／棧 | 模組數 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **I-harness** | `D:\I-harness-main`（`m62`） | TS ESM strict、零外部依賴 | 69 |
| 2 | **dsh** | `D:\agent-complete\deepseek-harness-dsh-v0.1.5-rc.2` | TS、Cordis 外掛 | 267 |
| 3 | **codex-rust** | `D:\agent-complete\codex-rust-v0.149.1` | Rust 2024 | 102 |
| 4 | **opencode** | `D:\agent-complete\opencode-1.18.30` | Bun ＋ Effect 4 | 97 |
| 5 | **opencode-fork** | `D:\agent-complete\opencode-fork-private-999.0.15` | 同上（fork） | 99 |
| 6 | **grok-build** | `D:\agent-complete\grok-build-main` | Rust | 82 |
| 7 | **cc-custom** | `D:\opencode-bugfix\cc-custom` | TS（Claude Code 系） | 36 |

**版本陷阱（實測）**：`opencode-fork-private-999.0.15` 的 `package.json` 是 **999.0.19**。目錄名不等於版本，本表一律採 manifest。

## 十二個域

| # | 域 | 涵蓋 |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `loop` | Agent 迴圈與輸入模型：turn/step 形狀、每 session 串行、輸入分級、steering、中斷 |
| 2 | `session` | 事件日誌與投影、replay、耐久性、崩潰修復、schema 遷移、所有權鎖 |
| 3 | `context` | 視窗解析、token 會計、壓縮策略、prune、影子投影、預算階梯與其失敗模式 |
| 4 | `tools` | registry 與 exposure、schema 驗證、並行執行與提交序、超時/重試、輸出溢出、patch、進程/PTY |
| 5 | `subagent` | 委派協定與耐久性、任務狀態機、取消樹、深度/併發配額、背景執行與父喚醒、團隊協同 |
| 6 | `safety` | OS 層圍堵後端與各自**實際**限制、讀寫隔離、審批流、危險分類、升級階梯、權限規則模型 |
| 7 | `model` | 線協議、模型目錄與發現、context/max-output 中介資料、思考強度轉譯、provider 解析鏈、憑據 |
| 8 | `extension` | MCP client/server、LSP、skills、hooks、plugins 與市場、workflow、排程、todo/goal |
| 9 | `service` | 對外消費者的傳輸面：HTTP/WS、串流與 replay、SDK wire 契約、ACP、app-server/RPC、服務自身認證 |
| 10 | `retrieval` | session 搜尋與 lineage、全文索引、跨會話記憶、codebase graph、檔案搜尋、附件/多模態 |
| 11 | `ops` | telemetry 與事件 manifest、診斷、成本與 token 會計、回饋通道、crash 處理、發行/更新 |
| 12 | `interface` | **僅限與後端的交界**：renderer 消費的事件／串流契約、螢幕模式政策、能力探測。渲染內部、layout、主題**不在範圍內** |

## 方法與驗證

1. **Phase 0（腳本，不經 LLM）**：`extract-mechanisms.mjs` 列出七源的每一個模組並依關鍵字歸域，產出 `2026-09-11-d3-modules.json`。分類器的提示**不是權威**——它被實測證明會在整個域裡把某個源判成空的（見下），所以各源代理必須更正它，更正內容列於附錄 B。
2. **Phase 1（六個代理，涵蓋七源）**：每源橫跨 12 域產出機制清單（機制／不變量／失敗策略／常數／`file:line`），**並逐一交代每個模組**。硬規則：**無 `file:line` 即標未驗證**。
3. **跨源對帳**：七個獨立代理對同一能力的命名必然分歧（「append-only 影子投影」vs「surface-replace 壓縮」）。硬用名字聯集會產生數百個單源列，讓矩陣**宣稱七個 harness 幾乎沒有共通點——那是方法造成的假象，不是事實**。因此列的來源是**顯式 crosswalk**，任何未被認領的機制**自成一路並進未對帳附錄**：寧可列出「這兩者可能同一個東西但我沒把握」，也不要靜默合併。
4. **對抗式驗證**：無利益關係的獨立代理抽樣重讀原文，判定引註是否真的支撐主張。

### Phase 0 分類器的四個缺陷（都已修，全部會讓某個源在整個域裡看起來是空的）

記錄於此，因為它們是「為什麼機器分類不足以做這件事、必須有人讀原始碼」的直接證據：

| 缺陷 | 後果 |
|---|---|
| 把 `codegen` 當成基建關鍵字 | grok 的 crate 都在 `crates/codegen/` 下，**82 個模組有 81 個被判成基建** |
| 用了裸 `core/` 關鍵字 | opencode 整個引擎（`packages/core/src` 的 26 個子模組）**全被歸進 agent loop** |
| `session` 只寫了複合詞（`core-session`、`session-persistence`…）**沒有裸 `session`** | 名叫 `core/session` 的模組**什麼都對不上**，整個 session 域在該源消失 |
| 子字串比對不吃複數 | `credentials` 對不上關鍵字 `credential`，**憑據處理從三個源消失** |

修正後：改為**詞段比對＋複數/動名詞容忍**，並把 test helper／util／schema 這類**基建另立一桶**，使「unmapped」代表「真的有能力模組沒歸位」。unmapped 從 **156 降至 32**（多為 `core`、`protocol` 這類容器模組），每個源橫跨 12–13 個域。

**證明強度聲明**：本盤點的所有機制主張來自**閱讀原始碼**，未執行任何程式碼。這是靜態審計，不是行為測試。

---

以下是機制矩陣本體。

## 圖例

| 符號 | 意義 |
|---|---|
| `✓` | 該源有此機制 |
| `✗` | 該源有此域，但沒有這個機制 |
| `—` | 該源在此域沒有對應層（見該域的說明） |
| `?` | 該源在此域的盤點清單為空——**「本次未盤點」而非「沒有此能力」**（見下方說明） |

> 每格顯示該源的機制摘要與**最佳可得**的 `file:line`。單源獨有的機制列於各表末尾並標 `(單源)`——它們未被任何 crosswalk 認領，**不表示其他源沒有等價能力**，只表示名稱未能對帳。

## 附錄 A — 模組覆蓋（每個源的每一個模組）

### IH（69 模組）

- `core-agent` — turn loop; four-tier input admission; per-session serial lane; ordered tool-batch commit lane; three-layer overflow ladder; abort semantics; the compaction consumer that is never wired
- `core-session` — append-only log + seq-by-append; deriveMessages wire-shape enforcement; the shadow-union projection; durable Inbox; image intake validation; runtime-context snapshot carrier (plugin-sourced user/message)
- `session-executor` — one assembly/two hosts + single-flight get-or-create; durable restore without re-append; queue/task projection; the `compact` pass-through that ships undefined
- `session-persistence` — coordinator load gate (version refusal before repair); write-behind with failure retention; tail-only log-semantic repair; ownership lease; fork with marker remapping; registerEventType vocabulary
- `session-persistence-jsonl` — JSONL durability (temp+fsync+rename, append rollback); structural repair (truncate + synthetic closers); path-traversal refusal; honest-bounding blank probe; header-only meta rewrite
- `session-query` — full-text session search + lineage over a rebuildable sqlite index; reconcile-on-search; FTS query safety by construction; node:sqlite warning suppression
- `session-title` — title derivation (deterministic fallback + one-shot provider suggestion), dual persistence (log event + document mirror), auto-title idempotence
- `runtime-context` — dynamic context as durable log snapshots; change-only append with an explicit cleared notice; replay-on-create
- `instructions` — AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md discovery precedence and byte-capped rendering with a sync stat-keyed cache
- `compaction` — append-only shadow markers; auto gate stack (pressure/sticky/re-fire/hysteresis/breaker); model-free prune pass; anchored summarization with verbatim sensitive instructions; catalog-first window resolution; the engine that no shipped host wires
- `token-meter` — single-projection token accounting (activeTokens/breakdown price deriveMessages); fixed-density estimator; inclusive budget check
- `output-retention` — two-layer size control: surrogate-safe byte-budget retainer + out-of-workspace spill store with a composing cascade guard and GC
- `rewind` — per-session journal + content-addressed pre-images; workspace binding refusal; lazy conflict classification and honest orphan reporting; read-only fail-soft git evidence probe
- `attachment` — opaque-id image attachment store with validate-before-publish, plus the read-only read_image tool
- `core-tools` — Tool contract + registry; three-stage dispatch; closed decision vocabulary; three-value exposure with search promotion; fail-closed approval seam; get_context_remaining registration gate
- `fs` — five workspace file tools: returned-failure model, TOCTOU re-check, CRLF-preserving edits, atomic writes, strict apply_patch parser, optional rewind pre-image sink, and NO sandbox integration
- `fs-lock` — session ownership lease as an OS byte-range lock with JS backoff and a fail-closed platform boundary
- `fs-search` — ripgrep-backed glob/grep with capped deferred result sets, explicit exit-code semantics, and no sandbox parameter
- `fs-watch` — chokidar stream as baseline-filtered deltas with readiness gating, a latched single error and segment-based ignores
- `shell` — bash/pwsh resolution honesty, argv view for the approval policy, opt-in retention with a spill bridge, and the OPTIONAL sandbox policy that makes shell the only confined tool family
- `exec` — process execution: per-spawn confinement resolution, runner-failure translation, tree-wide kill, foreground-only spill collector, background job registry
- `terminal` — PTY service with a ring-buffered re-readable offset log, per-PTY owner gating, service-side sizing and ConPTY noise filtering; no sandbox and no timeout on any tool
- `text-diff` — structured line diff with a work budget, position-aligned windowing that refuses skewed comparisons, and per-change-region hunk splitting
- `tool-search` — dependency-free BM25 engine with four selection modes (exact name, select: list, +required terms, natural language) and deterministic ranking
- `skills` — SKILL.md deferred retrieval: registry scan, strict YAML front-matter validation, BM25 summary search, gated implicit invocation with a shadow-selector telemetry report
- `lsp` — language-server integration: bounded framing, abortable serialized query queue, transient document lifecycles, fail-closed capability gating, four-step bounded teardown, and one-server-per-run reservation
- `mcp-client` — generation-based reconnect supervisor with backoff and an overlap guard; proxy-bound tool closures that fail fast; two-phase tool sync with phase-dependent conflict policy; OAuth 2.1 loopback flow with proactive fail-soft refresh; unambiguous public naming
- `workspace` — durable workspace registry over the coordinator document store with a single serialization chain for reads and writes, archive as a display layer, and a bounded symlink-free file walker
- `sandbox` — SandboxProvider contract (confine-or-throw), mode types, read-isolation capability gate, writable-root derivation, strictly-wider escalation ladder (no production caller), and the shared runner-failure classifier
- `sandbox-local` — platform-dispatch provider: bwrap profile on Linux, injected ACL backend on win32, fail-closed arms everywhere else, honest readIsolation:false declarations, and a real confined probe
- `sandbox-policy` — effective mode resolution (request > last sandbox/mode event > default) and the advisory policy text injected into the system prompt
- `sandbox-windows-acl` — WRITE_RESTRICTED token with mode-selected restricting SIDs, split workspace/temp capability SIDs, idempotent locked ACE grants, standing vs revocable grant lifecycle, and an argv runner that never spawns unrestricted
- `guard-approval` — three-layer approval policy, deny-on-metachar danger classifier, 'never' headless promotion, dual listener+waterfall seeding, and the LLM guardian reviewer with a fail-closed verdict and a deny breaker
- `guard-repeat-tool` — consecutive-identical-call counting with a threshold list and an internal nudge message (observational, never blocking)
- `guard-retry` — TOOL_TIMEOUT-only re-dispatch with backoff, a mount-order requirement, a redispatch marker and a WeakSet re-entrancy guard
- `guard-timeout` — per-tool timeout enforcement as a monotonic cascade guard; infrastructure-grade but it is the mechanism that turns a hang into TOOL_TIMEOUT
- `preset` — agent preset shape + validation, tools resolved before any scope mutation, child-scope mounting, and the default system prompt used as a base by the assembly
- `llm-seam` — provider-agnostic stream/request vocabulary, six-level effort passthrough, retry-only-before-output policy, cause-chain error classification, image projection for text-only routes, log-consistency assertion, transport error explanation
- `llm-anthropic` — Anthropic Messages wire adapter with generation-selected thinking protocols and header-collision discipline (it contributes no separate mechanism beyond the shared adapter set)
- `llm-openai` — OpenAI Responses wire adapter; shares the openai-family effort table and image-splitting discipline
- `llm-openai-compatible` — Chat Completions wire adapter with index-keyed tool-call accumulation and a synthesized call id
- `llm-gemini` — Gemini generateContent adapter with two generation rules, object-wrapped tool responses and name recovery from preceding assistant tool calls
- `llm-bedrock` — Bedrock Converse adapter with family-keyed reasoning fields and additionalModelRequestFields folding, over the AWS SDK
- `llm-mock` — scripted mock ModelClient for deterministic turn tests; reachable only through an explicit modelPolicy 'test-mock'
- `provider` — provider plane: registry-as-directory, unified model-context chain, model discovery probe with protocol-aware auth, response normalization, adapter dispatch, and the id-keyed websearch seam
- `provider-runtime` — settings+credentials-backed runtime: view merge (user > template), typed model resolution state machine, ref-based auth derivation, discovery cache with manual-row precedence and bedrock refusal
- `credentials` — ref-based credential store: refs-not-values, env-over-file precedence with shadowed-write rejection, symmetric emptiness stance, atomic 0600 writes, plaintext-at-rest with no keychain
- `core-plugin` — plugin/event kernel: scope tree, four extension channels with distinct contracts, monotonic ancestor composition, decision-slot race handling, and synchronous reclamation with an async cap
- `hooks` — user-configured hook handlers as spawned subprocesses: 9 events classified gate vs observer, per-invocation trust hashing, strict output validation, and explicit-config-vs-default existence semantics
- `plugin-registry` — host-facing plugin lifecycle: marketplace sources, atomic two-phase install, double-checked entry-source containment, materialized read-only overlays, re-keyed MCP config, and a pure capability evaluator that never executes plugin code
- `goal` — whole-snapshot goal mutations with a tombstone clear, pure planners, mandatory CAS on revision, typed error codes as the host contract, and a defensive read-side fold
- `jobs` — two-source job projection (durable snapshot doc + job/status fold) with a shared kill vocabulary and an honest 409 on an unknown target
- `schedule` — durable reminders event-sourced on the session log, three rule kinds, dispatch-as-acceptance ordering, injection-resistant reminder framing, and a frequency floor
- `subagent` — durable delegated-task protocol with identity CAS; notification outbox; crash recovery by classification, never re-dispatch; concurrency and depth quotas; the agent table and job registry
- `agent-team` — team scope: event-sourced roster/task board/mailbox on the lead log, pure-read serialized transactions, durability-before-ack delivery, lead-only authority, advisory write scopes, and tool replacement with a true unmount reverse
- `workflow` — static YAML workflows executed sequentially as one background job in a store that mirrors ExecService's contract (third layer of the job fallback chain)
- `todo` — whole-list todo snapshot tool with fail-loud validation and a pure last-event-wins projection
- `plan-mode` — plan mode as log state with a soft idempotent exit, an idempotently mounted exit tool, and a standalone prompt fragment
- `acp` — ACP v1 automation subset server with a binary permission face, an explicit close/generation lifecycle protocol, and a documented v0 drop-set
- `sdk` — external stdio SDK: NDJSON JSON-RPC 2.0 framing, a frozen v0 field-level contract with additive-only versioning, capability rows, honest blank listing, and per-session abort slots
- `interaction` — host/engine interaction seams: boolean-normalized approval answerer, synchronous fail-closed question provider, shared command grammar, and a UI-only command plane
- `web-host` — web service surface: unary HTTP routes + one WebSocket mux with refcounted live bundles, unconditional DNS-rebind/CORS fence, opt-in auth, single-workspace policy with an advisory cwd warning, and honest 404/409/500 mappings
- `web` — external-content tool surface: webfetch (http/https only, bounded head-tail extraction, size-capped body read) and websearch (registered only when an id-keyed websearch provider is usable, so the tool is ABSENT rather than failing at call time), both read-only/concurrency-safe with a 30 s timeout, both carrying the EXTERNAL_WEB_CONTENT_NOTICE trust-boundary marker as a distinct envelope FIELD rather than concatenated into content (packages/web/src/index.ts:12-13, packages/web/src/index.ts:45-93, packages/web/src/index.ts:122-124) — see the tools-domain mechanism external-content-tool-surface
- `telemetry` — host-side telemetry stream: closed manifest of 20 codes with cross-source refs, multicasting emitter with sink-error isolation, stdout JSONL sink
- `feedback` — per-message feedback store with per-item version CAS, a durable-log-verified message identity, and verify-after-write durability
- `settings` — layered settings document with raw-document merge, master-source writes, polling hot reload gated on normalized change, comment preservation with degradation, and a section leaf-patch protocol with revisions
- `cli` — CLI host surface: subcommand dispatch, five hosts over ONE assembly, and a split fail-loud/silent-fallback flag discipline
- `tui` — embedded backend bridge (session-log subscription mapped to the renderer event vocabulary with 16 ms batching), the hard slash-capability gate, screen-mode relaunch seam, and layout/presentation options — the rendering internals themselves are out of scope
- `tui-core` — terminal/engine primitives consumed by the TUI bridge (input decoding, frame engine, capability probe) — infrastructure for the backend interface, no separate capability claim

### dsh（267 模組）

- `compaction/command-compact` — dsh: compaction-engine-seam (manual /compact entry point registering through ctx.commands and calling ctx.compaction.compactNow); compaction-durable-bracket-transaction (maps each ManualCompactionError code to human text and returns summarySeq as sourceEventSeq)

- `extensions/cordis-host-runner` — extension: dynamic-cordis-package-sandbox-approval-and-client-execution (host half: registry, vm sandbox, guarded fiber lifecycle, approval gate, @Remote operations)

- `util/atomic-write` — tools: atomic-write-and-cross-process-locking — a standalone utility, independent of the fs domain: `writeFileAtomic` with a `wx` random-suffix sibling plus Windows rename retries, and `withFileLock` for cross-process writer serialization. Also contributes a second, distinct writer used by `file-tool-suite-with-observation-policy` callers outside this package.
- `util/brand` — service: support library only (nominal primitive helpers); contributes to no mechanism \| ops: support library only; contributes to no ops mechanism \| interface: support library only; contributes to no interface mechanism
- `util/chunked-list` — service: support library only (persistent append-only lists, CHUNK_CAPACITY 64); contributes to no named mechanism \| ops: support library only; contributes to no ops mechanism \| interface: support library only; contributes to no interface mechanism
- `util/crypto` — session: Contributes none to the session domain: randomUUID/bytesToBase64 serve gateway/client/inspector/llm message-id minting; the session packages use node:crypto directly (session-persistence-jsonl hashing/temp names) or brand their own ids. It is stateless utility code with no session-log, locking, or durability role.
- `util/deque` — service: support library only (circular deque used by the gateway and remotes queues); contributes to no named mechanism \| ops: support library only; contributes to no ops mechanism \| interface: support library only; contributes to no interface mechanism

Seam-plus-vocabulary, not pure plumbing: the portability rules are real design surface.
- `code-runtime/code-runtime-worker-thread` — tools: code-runtime-isolation — the shipped typescript backend; worker spawn with resource limits, hostile-peer frame re-validation, the JSON byte ledger and the binding bridge (packages/code-runtime/code-runtime-worker-thread/src/index.ts:136-247, bootstrap.ts:385-423).
- `core/tools` — tools: tool-registry-and-scoped-visibility; tool-schema-dsl-and-output-contract; tool-execution-pipeline-and-guards; tool-presentation-modes-and-ptc-collapse; cooperative-cancellation-classification; parallel-tool-execution-with-commit-ordering (the scheduler interface). `index.ts` and `ptc.ts` carry the logic; `types.ts` and `presentation.ts` are event/render vocabulary; `ts-types.ts`/`py-types.ts` are SDK renderers; `testing.ts` is a test-only fixture factory; `invariant.ts` is the package-owned invariant companion consumed through `tool-execution-pipeline-and-guards`.
- `e2b/fs-e2b` — tools: e2b-remote-execution-backends — `E2BFileSystem extends FileSystem` with `static inject = ['e2b']`; no Cordis service of its own. NO production caller: mounted only by packages/e2b/e2b/tests/fixtures/composition/cordis.yml:19.
- `e2b/subprocess-e2b` — tools: e2b-remote-execution-backends — `E2BSubprocessRuntime extends SubprocessRuntime` with `static inject = ['e2b']`; real provider logic (remote executable resolution, NUL validation, `pollMs` default 20, terminal setup tracking, disposal). NO production caller found in-tree.
- `experimental/code-runtime-python` — tools: code-runtime-isolation — the CPython backend (process isolation, fd-3 protocol, resource limits, kill escalation). `package.json:41` marks it `"private": true` and only snapshots/session/ptc-python-turn/cordis.yml:29-30 loads it, so it has NO shipped production caller.
- `extensions/tool-cordis` — tools: tool-registry-and-scoped-visibility — a CONSUMER/producer, not a new mechanism: `apply()` registers a `tool:cordis` prompt section, seven `defineTool` entries (cordis_inspect_list/query/self, cordis_define, cordis_run, cordis_stop, cordis_undefine) and one `agent/pre-step` listener (packages/extensions/tool-cordis/src/index.ts:25-61, 502). `api-catalog.ts` (6616 lines) is generated code and was excluded. \| extension: dynamic-cordis-package-sandbox-approval-and-client-execution (model-facing cordis_inspect_* / define / run / stop surface and inspect-provider registration)
- `fs/fs` — tools: file-tool-suite-with-observation-policy — the abstract `FileSystem` service seam at `ctx.fs`: 13 abstract members, three events (`fs/write-intent`, `fs/edit-intent`, `fs/observed`), the `sandboxMode` capability fact and the 13-literal `FsErrorCode` union. Contract-only: no filesystem I/O of its own.
- `fs/fs-local` — tools: file-tool-suite-with-observation-policy (realpath target identity, CAS write/edit guards, per-target lock, synced staged atomic write, hard-link no-clobber create, CRLF-normalised literal edit, bounded diff basis, stable listings) and atomic-write-and-cross-process-locking (its own `handle.sync()`-based `writeFileAtomic`, distinct from the util package's).
- `fs/fs-observation-policy` — tools: file-tool-suite-with-observation-policy — the read-before-write gate. An event-only plugin that registers NO service and has no `inject`; it holds the `WeakMap<owner, Map<targetKey, FsObservation>>` state and occupies both intent waterfalls, so it is real policy, not plumbing.
- `fs/fs-sandbox` — tools: filesystem-and-shell-sandboxing — `SandboxedFileSystem` fences only `writeText`/`editText` per call, plus the lexical-then-identity containment test in `containment.ts`. Cordis plumbing is limited to `inject = ['sandboxPolicy']` and the `sandboxMode` override. \| safety: fs-mutation-containment-fence
- `fs/tool-fs` — tools: file-tool-suite-with-observation-policy — the model-facing `read`/`write`/`edit`/`read_image` suite: schemas, argument validation, read windowing and rendering, session-cwd resolution, sandbox escalation controller, error remediation, image sniffer/attachment commit and result presentation meta. Holds no filesystem implementation.
- `fs/tool-fs-search` — tools: search-tools-and-native-command-resolution — ripgrep argv construction, complete-stdout parsing, per-line/per-item retention, search-meta byte bounding, formatted spill and the timeout/grace/stderr budget declarations. Deliberately does not inject `fs`. \| extension: ripgrep-backed-file-search-tools \| retrieval: ripgrep-backed-file-search-tools
- `fs/tool-present` — tools: file-tool-suite-with-observation-policy — the `present` delivery tool (open-turn + cwd + 1..`maxFiles` regular-file validation, durable `deliverables/presented` event only for a non-error result). Small but real logic; Cordis surface is one `tools.register` plus one `tools/result` listener.
- `fs/tool-str-replace-editor` — tools: string-replace-patch-editing — one multi-command editor tool with its own absolute-path rule, unique-literal-match logic, line insertion, shallow listing, output clipping and a duplicated `MutationPolicy` sandbox split.
- `goal/tool-goal` — subagent: goal-human-authority-and-command-surface
- `guard/repeat-tool-reminder` — tools: repeat-tool-call-reminder-guard — Cordis plugin (name/Config/apply with one `tools/post-execute` listener and one `agent/pre-step` reset hook) that registers no service; detection, canonicalization and reminder composition are real logic. \| safety: advisory-repeat-tool-guard
- `interaction/tool-ask-user` — safety: human-question-tool-seam
- `jobs/tool-jobs` — subagent: background-job-registry-and-parent-wake
- `lsp/tool-lsp` — extension: lsp-model-facing-tool-and-result-caps
- `shell/bash-local` — tools: shell-tools-and-persistent-sessions — the POSIX shell executor: request resolution and clamping, the fused per-run deadline with `BASH_TIMEOUT` classification, collect-mode output with spill, background `ShellProcess` handles and the rendered exit contract.
- `shell/bash-sandbox` — tools: filesystem-and-shell-sandboxing — `SandboxBashExecutor extends LocalBashExecutor` with `inject = ['subprocess','sandbox','sandboxPolicy']` and no own Config. NOT a thin wrapper: besides delegating the argv wrap to `ctx.sandbox.confine(['bash','-c',cmd], policy)`, it owns a per-process `processFacts` Map keyed by ShellProcess, the `danger-full-access` short-circuit, `SandboxUnavailableError` translation for foreground runner failures, `runnerFailed` stamping plus denial classification in `onProcessDone`, and ~116 lines of ENOENT/EACCES argv[0] attribution and rule-matching helpers. \| safety: shell-confinement-wrap-and-denial-classification
- `shell/pwsh-local` — tools: shell-tools-and-persistent-sessions — the PowerShell executor, sharing the `'BASH_TIMEOUT'` code string with bash-local so both shells speak one timeout dialect, plus the encoding preamble and pwsh executable resolution.
- `shell/pwsh-sandbox` — tools: filesystem-and-shell-sandboxing — the pwsh twin of bash-sandbox, declared in code as a deliberate call-for-call mirror (class body wrapped in `jscpd:ignore-start`/`ignore-end`, helpers header says 'deliberate call-for-call mirror'). Its only pwsh-specific contribution is confining `this.argv(spec)` instead of a `bash -c` string. Real logic, not a thin wrapper. \| safety: shell-confinement-wrap-and-denial-classification (deliberate call-for-call mirror of the bash executor)
- `shell/shell` — tools: shell-tools-and-persistent-sessions — abstract `ShellExecutor` service seam at `ctx.shell`; it declares the resolve/run/start contract and `sandboxMode` but implements nothing. Mostly a service definition, with real documented invariants (`run` rejects only on infrastructure failure, background processes are stopped at composition teardown).
- `shell/shell-env` — tools: shell-tools-and-persistent-sessions — `ShellEnvRegistry` at `ctx.shellEnv` builds the `DSH_*` reserved environment keys and validates contributor-supplied `BASH_ENV`-style names; a small registry rather than a mechanism of its own, consumed by the shell tools.
- `shell/tool-bash` — tools: shell-tools-and-persistent-sessions — the model-facing `bash` tool (`command`, `description`, `timeoutMs`, `workdir`, conditional `run_in_background`, conditional escalation fields), job-start wiring, workdir resolution and the terminal-card presentation/exit-pill parsing.
- `shell/tool-bash-persistent` — tools: shell-tools-and-persistent-sessions — the persistent PTY bash tool: per-agent session registry, nonce start/end markers, owner-serialized command queue and scrollback paging. It depends only on `ctx.terminals`, NOT `ctx.shell`.
- `shell/tool-pwsh` — tools: shell-tools-and-persistent-sessions — the PowerShell mirror of tool-bash (same registration shape, escalation gating and presentation contract).
- `shell/tool-pwsh-persistent` — tools: shell-tools-and-persistent-sessions — the persistent PTY pwsh tool, mirroring tool-bash-persistent with its own prompt sentinel and echo stripping.
- `skill/skill-filesystem` — extension: filesystem-skill-discovery-and-watch-invalidation
- `skill/tool-skill` — extension: skill-catalog-injection-and-model-facing-loader
- `spill/spill` — tools: output-spill-with-preview-and-locator — the abstract `SpillStore` service seam at `ctx.spillStore` with a single `saveText` member and the `SpillRef`/`SaveTextSpill` vocabulary. Service definition plus contract; no storage mechanics.
- `spill/spill-local` — tools: output-spill-with-preview-and-locator — `LocalSpillStore` session-scoped private writes (0700 dir, `wx` 0600 file, traversal-safe name encoding) and the best-effort startup cleanup sweep.
- `spill/spill-policy` — tools: output-spill-with-preview-and-locator — the two `tools/post-execute` / `tools/ptc-dispatch-log` arms, the notice budget reservation and the browser-safe `formatSpillNotice`/`hasSpillNotice` formatter. Cordis plugin registering no service.
- `subagent/tool-subagent` — subagent: named-provider-registry-and-capability-gate \| one-shot-run-lifecycle-and-settlement \| delegation-depth-quota \| subagent-model-selection-policy \| continuable-activation-residency-and-child-lock \| background-job-registry-and-parent-wake
- `subagent/tool-subagent-control` — subagent: subagent-control-and-messaging-surfaces \| durable-child-catalog-and-discovery-listing
- `subprocess/subprocess` — tools: pty-terminal-sessions and subprocess-spawn-and-process-tree-control — abstract `SubprocessRuntime` service seam at `ctx.subprocess` plus the canonical child environment scrub (`scrubbedParentEnv`, `SENSITIVE_ENV_PATTERN`) and the terminal-spawn contract. Not pure plumbing: the env scrub is real shared behavior.
- `subprocess/subprocess-local` — tools: subprocess-spawn-and-process-tree-control (spawn, containment-mode selection, termination ladder, tail-keep output collection with spill, native execve bootstrap, win32 Job runner) and pty-terminal-sessions (`spawnTerminal` through node-pty, `/proc`-based process inspection, terminal teardown).
- `subprocess/win32-process` — tools: subprocess-spawn-and-process-tree-control — the koffi FFI layer: lazy DLL binding, kill-on-close Job creation, suspended create/assign/resume, command-line and environment encoding, anonymous-pipe capture, Job polling and Win32 error-code translation. Has NO Cordis Service, `apply` or Context augmentation at all (`index.ts` is a pure re-export barrel), so there is no plumbing here to note.
- `terminal/terminal` — tools: pty-terminal-sessions — `TerminalSessionService` at `ctx.terminals`: backend registry, exact-Agent session records (ids minted as `pty-${++nextId}`), reserved names, pending-spawn tracking with rollback, owner cleanups, 8 machine-routable `TerminalErrorCode`s and a memoized `disposeAll`. A real in-process registry, not a mere service declaration.
- `terminal/terminal-bash` — tools: pty-terminal-sessions — the default PTY backend: headless terminal emulator, bounded scrollback, prompt-marker sanitizer and the settled-operation protocol with explicit wait reasons.
- `terminal/tool-terminal` — tools: pty-terminal-sessions — the model-facing terminal tool suite (`terminal_open`/`send`/`read`/`signal`/`close`/`list`) with `maxResultBytes` (256 KiB default) result bounding and background-task output.
- `todo/tool-todo` — subagent: todo-list-write-and-fold
- `web/tool-web` — extension: web-search-provider-selection-and-tool-bounds (model-facing web_search/web_fetch schemas, bounds and presentation) \| retrieval: web-search-provider-selection-and-tool-bounds
- `webhook/webhook-github` — service: webhook-ingress-and-verification
- `workflow/tool-ralph` — subagent: workflow-orchestration-engine-and-worker-containment
- `workflow/tool-workflow` — subagent: workflow-orchestration-engine-and-worker-containment
- `workspace/workspace` — tools: workspace-resolution-and-file-apis — the durable `WorkspaceRegistry` at `ctx.workspaceRegistry` (canonical-path uniqueness, versioned storage domain with a replayed `pendingMutation` marker, header-validated session membership, serialized operations). Substantive domain logic; the Cordis surface is the service registration plus a domain-cache invariant companion.

- `sandboxing` — safety/platform-sandbox-selection-and-transform, seatbelt-and-bubblewrap-backends, sandbox-violation-classification; also tools/orchestration (SandboxManager is the orchestrator's sandbox selector)
- `scripts` — none (build tooling only): a single PowerShell setup script (setup-windows.ps1); zero source files
- `secrets` — model/auth-persistence-backend-and-unauthorized-recovery and retrieval/two-phase-memory-pipeline-with-lease-queue (secret redaction before rollout transcripts reach memory extraction) and safety (encrypted secret storage)
- `shell-command` — tools/unified-pty-exec-process-execution and exec-policy: command parsing, shell detection, shell snapshot and `is_dangerous_command` safety classification (shell-command/src/lib.rs:1-11)
- `shell-escalation` — tools/approval-sandbox-retry-orchestrator and safety: the Unix shell escalation protocol (EscalateServer/EscalationPolicy/ShellCommandExecutor) used when a shell reruns a command with escalated permissions
- `skills` — extension/layered-skill-catalog-developer-injection and explicit-mention-and-implicit-skill-invocation — mention parsing, selection, skill model, invocation observation
- `state` — session/six-database-sqlite-state-runtime, durable-queued-user-submissions; ops/sqlite-log-database-and-diagnostics; retrieval (memory and thread-history runtime queries); subagent/goal-durable-continuation and thread-spawn-topology-graph-store
- `stdio-to-uds` — service/app-server-multi-transport-listen-surface — bridges stdio to a Unix domain socket so a client can speak the control-socket protocol
- `terminal-detection` — interface/screen-mode-and-terminal-capability-detection — terminal and multiplexer classification from environment variables
- `test-binary-support` — none (test infrastructure): a 1-file crate supporting test binaries; no production capability
- `thread-manager-sample` — none (example): a single-file sample main demonstrating the ThreadManager API; no production capability
- `thread-store` — session/rollout-lineage-and-compression and durable-queued-user-submissions (QueueStore trait); retrieval/rollout-content-search-with-ripgrep-fallback and thread-history-occurrence-search-pagination
- `tools` — tools/tool-registry-with-exposure-ladder (ToolName/ToolSpec/ToolExposure/ToolExecutor contracts) and tool discovery (TOOL_SEARCH_TOOL_NAME, DiscoverableTool)
- `tui` — interface/renderer-notification-routing-and-bounded-replay, screen-mode-and-terminal-capability-detection, slash-command-capability-gating; ops/crash-handling panic hooks (tui/src/lib.rs:975-988, tui/src/tui.rs:551-557)
- `uds` — service/app-server-multi-transport-listen-surface — Unix domain socket helpers for the control socket and exec-server transport
- `utils` — infrastructure (80 files across ~25 sub-crates): output-truncation (shared TruncationPolicy used by every tool), pty (PTY spawning for unified exec), image (prompt image decoding/resizing and the 1 GiB input cap), stream-parser (assistant text/plan segment parsing), approval-presets (the read-only/auto/full-access presets behind /permissions), absolute-path/path-uri (path newtypes used by the permission model), plus cli/cache/elapsed/fuzzy-match/home-dir/json-to-toml/oss/plugins/readiness/rustls-provider/sandbox-summary/sleep-inhibitor/string/template/audio. Contributes shared primitives rather than capabilities of its own.
- `v8-poc` — none (proof of concept): a Bazel-wired crate that only reports the embedded V8 version and whether V8 was built with its in-process sandbox; reserved for future V8 experiments (v8-poc/src/lib.rs:1-24)
- `vendor` — safety/seatbelt-and-bubblewrap-backends (supporting): vendored bubblewrap C sources (bind-mount.c, bubblewrap.c, network.c, utils.c) compiled for the Linux sandbox's filesystem isolation
- `websocket-client` — model/responses-websocket-transport-with-https-fallback — the WebSocket client used by the Responses-over-WebSocket transport
- `windows-sandbox-rs` — safety/windows-sandbox-three-levels-with-wfp — restricted-token and elevated backends, ACL/deny-read resolution, DPAPI-stored offline sandbox user, WFP filter specifications and the unified-exec session spawner
- `workload-identity` — model/provider-credential-resolution-chain (supporting): workload-identity credentials for hosted deployments; the MCP server refuses to start when workload identity is selected (mcp-server/src/lib.rs:211-219)

### grok（83 模組）

- `xai-grok-sandbox` — OS-level confinement: startup-applied Landlock/Seatbelt via nono, bwrap re-exec with read-deny placeholders, built-in profile set with the additive-only project merge, hook write-deny planning, per-spawn seccomp child-network filter, sandbox-config loading. Contributes mechanisms to `safety` only.
- `xai-grok-config` — The managed-policy plumbing: six-layer config precedence ending in macOS MDM, root-owned requirements.toml loading and merging, fail_closed arming, Ed25519 signed-policy-at-rest verification, probe helpers for the policy files. Contributes the managed-policy, MDM, signed-policy and managed-hooks mechanisms to `safety`; the layer machinery itself is infrastructure for every domain that reads config.
- `xai-grok-config-types` — The `[features]` registry and the seven-tier `BoolFlag` precedence ladder (requirement > cli > env > config > managed > remote > default). Supplies the fail-closed-by-default feature gate used by the enterprise/platform gating mechanisms in `safety`.
- `xai-prompt-queue` — Pure merge rules for queued follow-ups (`combine_prefix_len`, `can_merge_front/follower`, blank-line join, combined-display meta) plus the `QueueEntryWire`/`QueueChanged` broadcast contract. Contributes `queued-prompt-combine-with-versioned-edit` to `loop` and `queue-state-broadcast-contract` to `interface`.
- `xai-agent-lifecycle` — Host-agnostic agent lifecycle hook dispatch: data-only per-hook inputs, capabilities injected at install time, contributors never own loop control. Extends the loop's turn boundaries; no capability claim of its own beyond the hook-dispatch seam.
- `xai-grok-agent` — Shared agent object: builder, definition parsing, system-prompt assembly, compaction and system-reminder policy bundled into one type any host can consume. Host-independent half of the loop/context configuration.
- `xai-grok-shell` — The main host binary's library. Contributes directly to `loop` (message admission and identity checks, parent-message lifecycle, interjection buffer, queue metadata), `subagent` (parent wake, goal-role capability pinning), `safety` (the fail-closed `apply_sandbox` startup path, the permission-mode dispatcher) and `interface` (the per-session command-availability snapshot).
- `xai-grok-shell-base` — Foundation modules extracted out of xai-grok-shell so they build in parallel; shell re-exports them at their original paths. Infrastructure for the same capabilities as xai-grok-shell.
- `xai-grok-shell-terminal` — Local, ACP and PTY terminal runners for the shell; re-exported as `xai_grok_shell::terminal`. Tool-execution substrate for the `tools` domain.
- `xai-grok-shell-session-support` — Session-support modules lifted out of the shell's `session/` tree (e.g. `managed_mcp`) purely so they stop rebuilding on shell edits. Infrastructure, no separate capability.
- `xai-grok-tools` — Tool registry; tool config and ToolKind-based filtering; the subagent task tool. Contributes `harness-only-subagent-capability-mode` to `subagent`, and is the registry side of the `tools` domain.
- `xai-grok-tools-api` — Shared API definitions for tools: protobuf types with per-field serde defaults for the external JSON contracts, config validation, canonical slash-command wording. Wire contract for the `tools` domain, and the reason `tools` should not be called absent here.
- `xai-grok-workspace` — The workspace service: permission resolution across every config source, the auto-mode permission classifier, managed MCP policy. Contributes the always-approve policy pin to `safety` (see `root-owned-managed-policy-pin`).
- `xai-grok-workspace-types` — Pure-data wire types for the workspace API; no tokio, no I/O, deliberately cheap to depend on including from a future WASM SDK. Service-domain contract.
- `xai-grok-workspace-client` — Typed client for hub-proxied `workspace.*` RPC methods; the single transport for the `workspace_rpc` channel. Service-domain client side.
- `xai-grok-workspace-daemon` — Daemonization (double-fork + setsid, single-instance pidfile lock) and preview-proxy supervision for the standalone workspace server. Every daemon-owned file is opened `O_NOFOLLOW` and mode 0600. Service-domain process plumbing.
- `xai-grok-http` — Shared `reqwest` client construction: four clients (cached async general, cached upload with aggressive pool eviction, blocking startup client, on-demand pool-less HTTP/1.1 for escaping a poisoned pool). Infrastructure underneath the `service` and `ops` domains; contributes no mechanism of its own. The classifier left this one `unmapped`.
- `xai-acp-lib` — The ACP transport itself: agent/client channel pair, gateway sender/receiver, message boxing, line-buffered and stdin readers, normalization. This is the Agent Client Protocol wire layer, so it belongs to `service` rather than the `extension` hint.
- `xai-grok-session-events` — The canonical per-turn event taxonomy and its JSONL writer: `EVENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`, the tagged `Event` enum, `EventWriter`, `EventTracker`. Contributes `canonical-turn-event-log` to `loop` and `append-only-jsonl-event-writer` to `session`.
- `xai-chat-state` — Chat/conversation state projection over the pure sampling types. Session-domain state carrier.
- `xai-grok-active-sessions` — Crash-recovery registry of open sessions in `active_sessions.json` with a separate lock file and temp-file write. Contributes `crash-recovery-active-session-registry` to `session`.
- `xai-grok-foreign-sessions` — Bounded, metadata-only listing of other agents' session stores, capped at 50 sessions/tool, 30 days, 200 title chars. Contributes `bounded-metadata-only-foreign-session-scan` to `session`.
- `xai-grok-dashboard-store` — SQLite-backed dashboard workspace store (membership, layout ranks, grouping) with an explicit ownership contract: this crate owns every byte of that SQL and consumers get only the typed API. Session-domain persistence for a UI-facing feature.
- `xai-sqlite-journal` — Filesystem-aware SQLite journal-mode selection (WAL on local, TRUNCATE + per-host DB file on network mounts) to avoid the NFS `-shm` SIGBUS. Contributes `filesystem-aware-sqlite-journal-mode` to `session`.
- `xai-compaction-transcript` — Pure, I/O-free rendering of a compacted segment into self-contained markdown with a byte-capped segment size and a truncation notice. Contributes `compaction-transcript-rendering-with-byte-caps` to `context`.
- `xai-token-estimation` — The single source of truth for context arithmetic: bytes/4 heuristic, flat per-image estimate, threshold predicates and percentage helpers shared by `/context`, auto-compact gates and the preflight overflow check. Contributes `single-source-token-estimation` to `context`.
- `xai-codebase-graph` — Tree-sitter-based code graph over the repository: go-to-definition, go-to-references, full initial index build. Retrieval-domain index.
- `xai-grok-memory` — Cross-session memory as human-editable markdown under `~/.grok/memory/`, one global file plus a per-workspace subdirectory. Retrieval-domain store; also supplies the always-on `memory_search`/`memory_get` tools whose presence gates the memory commands in `interface`.
- `xai-grok-session-search` — Full-text search over local sessions backed by a rebuildable SQLite FTS5 cache with BM25 ranking, quarantine-and-recreate recovery for an unusable DB file, and a lease ensuring one process reindexes at a time. Retrieval, not session - the classifier's `session` hint reflects the directory it indexes rather than the capability.
- `xai-fuzzy-file-search` — Fuzzy path search over a directory tree (`ignore` walk feeding a `nucleo` matcher, driven from a background thread with poll-based top-k). Degrades per stage rather than aborting. Retrieval-domain.
- `xai-workflow` — Workflow engine with journal, meta, validate and run modules plus a host interface (`MAX_WORKFLOW_NAME_LEN = 64`). Extension-domain per the taxonomy, which places workflow engines under `extension`.
- `xai-grok-plugin-marketplace` — Marketplace source configuration and plugin discovery, indexed with a filesystem fallback; install integration goes through the existing InstallRegistry pipeline. Extension-domain.
- `xai-grok-hooks` — The runtime hook system: file-based discovery, command and HTTP handlers, sequential gate dispatch with Deny > Ask > Defer > Allow precedence, per-gate-kind timeout budgets, provenance/trust ranking and the managed-policy no-disable exemption. Contributes `hooks-as-fail-open-gates-with-deny-precedence` to `safety`; the extension plumbing is the mechanism in `extension`.
- `xai-hooks-plugins-types` — Dependency-free serde DTOs defining the `x.ai/hooks/*` and `x.ai/plugins/*` ACP extension wire format. Wire contract supporting the hooks and plugin mechanisms.
- `xai-grok-extra-ca` — TLS root policy: OS roots plus Mozilla roots plus opt-in extra roots from `GROK_EXTRA_CA_BUNDLE` (fallback `SSL_CERT_FILE`), with rustls pinned across every client. Credentials/model-adjacent transport trust, not an extension surface - the classifier's `extension` hint is wrong.
- `xai-grok-mcp` — MCP client, structured around quarantining `rmcp` 2.1 / `reqwest` 0.13 as a fully private implementation detail of the `servers` module so the rest of the workspace stays on reqwest 0.12. Extension-domain, with the quirk that a large part of the crate exists to contain a dependency-version split.
- `xai-grok-secrets` — Secret redaction: `redact_secrets`, `redact_json_string_values`, `redact_url`, `redact_user_paths` and a JSON walker. A data-handling capability; the `safety` hint is defensible but it is not a permission or sandbox mechanism.
- `xai-grok-sampler` — Actor-based sampling layer: HTTP streaming and retry logic with a layered API over a shared HTTP/2 pooled client and a pool-less HTTP/1.1 fallback, reading `GROK_POOL_*` and `GROK_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECS` once at first build. Model-domain transport.
- `xai-grok-sampling-types` — Pure data types for the sampling API: conversation, request/response, streaming and error types, with no I/O. Model-domain contract.
- `xai-grok-auth` — Auth traits shared between the credential holder (`xai-file-utils`) and the shell, including refresh-aware token resolution, bearer fragments and an optional retry middleware. Keeps shell types out of the data-collector's import graph. Model-domain credentials.
- `xai-grok-models` — Default model IDs loaded from an embedded `default_models.json`, resolved as CLI flag > env > config.toml > remote settings > these defaults. Model-domain catalogue.
- `xai-grok-env` — Backend environment presets: endpoint URL defaults and environment selection, resolving a `GROK_*` env override over a compiled production default. Public builds expose production endpoints. Configuration infrastructure for model and service.
- `xai-system-power` — Cross-platform sleep/wake notifications with a documented motivation: an OIDC refresh in flight when the laptop sleeps can lose its rotated successor token, so the auth manager consumes these events to avoid starting a refresh just before sleep and may block a `WillSleep` handler briefly to let an in-flight refresh finish. Unmapped by the classifier; it is a model/credentials mechanism.
- `xai-grok-bundle` — On-disk cache for the published subagent bundle (personas, roles, agents, skills, workflows) under `<grok home>/bundled`, with a checksum manifest so a hand-edited file is never overwritten or pruned, bounded archive extraction and path sanitization before joining. Extension-domain distribution.
- `xai-grok-telemetry` — Telemetry pipeline and event emission used by the shell and pager. Ops-domain.
- `xai-tracing-macros` — `tprintln!`/`teprintln!`/`timed!` logging and timing macros. Ops-domain infrastructure.
- `xai-grok-update` — Self-update machinery for the CLI. Ops-domain distribution.
- `xai-grok-version` — The installed CLI version string, kept in sync with the shipping binaries. Ops-domain metadata.
- `xai-crash-handler` — Crash-handler installation and reporting. Ops-domain.
- `xai-mixpanel` — Minimal Mixpanel `track`-only client, written to use reqwest 0.12 and avoid a second HTTP stack in the binary. Ops-domain analytics sink.
- `xai-grok-announcements` — Shared announcement (banner notification) types, persistence and formatting for the shell and pager. Ops/interface-adjacent broadcast.
- `xai-grok-gboom` — `/gboom`: a single-level raycaster rendered through the kitty graphics protocol, streaming PNG frames in a modal overlay. The crate's own doc says it is not production code and is not maintained to the repo's standards. Ops-domain easter egg.
- `xai-grok-diag-server` — In-guest diagnostics HTTP server (`/ready`, `/statusz`, `/logs`) for the standalone workspace server: loopback-only TCP or a 0600 Unix socket, never exposed through the sandbox port mapping, with `/logs` returning the raw daemon log. Service-domain, with an explicit warning that log output is sensitive.
- `xai-grok-status-line` — The status-line contract: `config` is what a user writes in `[ui.status_line]`, `context` is what the agent sends clients to draw. A leaf crate upstream of the agent and every client, so it is a backend-interface seam rather than rendering internals.
- `xai-grok-voice` — Voice input: an xAI streaming STT client plus the pipeline task emitting `VoiceEvent`s. Dictation only - the transcript lands in the prompt box. Interface-adjacent backend service; the mic is opened in a short-lived subprocess on macOS/Linux so the long-lived TUI does not pay the audio stack's permanent memory cost.
- `xai-fast-worktree` — High-performance git worktree creation using copy-on-write cloning. Tool substrate.
- `xai-fsnotify` — Local-filesystem event source: a single causal stream of wire-ready `FsEvent`s on one broadcast channel, single workspace root only, with multi-root composition pushed up to the workspace layer. Tool substrate.
- `xai-gix-status` — Shared helpers for `gix` status scans, capping the in_parallel thread limit because a failed scoped spawn under `panic=abort` and a tight RLIMIT_NPROC aborts the whole process rather than yielding a recoverable JoinError. Tool substrate.
- `xai-hunk-tracker` — Actor-based tracking of file hunks with Agent vs External attribution, integrated with shell sessions. Tool substrate.
- `ptyctl` — Headless PTY controller built on alacritty_terminal: spawn processes in a PTY, send keystrokes, read screen content as text/styled/HTML, expose it all over an HTTP REST API. Tool substrate for terminal control.
- `ptyctl-cli` — CLI front end for the ptyctl headless PTY controller. Tool substrate.
- `xai-grok-pager` — The full-screen pager application. Contributes the permission-mode projection and queue drain to `safety`, the screen-mode switch and its in-process reseed to `interface`, and the command-source model to the command surface. The remainder is TUI application and rendering, which domain 12 excludes.
- `xai-grok-pager-bin` — Binary entry point for the pager; main.rs only. Carries the requested-confinement warning that disables leader mode under a confining profile, and otherwise contributes no engine capability.
- `xai-grok-pager-minimal` — Minimal, scrollback-native render mode (`grok --minimal`): finalized blocks are printed once into the terminal's native scrollback via `xai_ratatui_inline::Terminal::insert_before`, with a small pinned live region and no interactive ScrollbackPane. Rendering internals, outside domain 12's backend-interface limit; the mode-switch policy half lives in `xai-grok-pager`.
- `xai-grok-pager-diff` — Turns edit-tool output into line-tagged `DiffHunk`s and back into unified-diff text. Presentation-layer diff construction.
- `xai-grok-pager-pty-harness` — PTY-based end-to-end harness that drives the real pager binary. Test infrastructure.
- `xai-grok-pager-render` — Rendering crate for the pager: layout, theming, widget drawing. Rendering internals, explicitly out of scope for domain 12.
- `xai-grok-markdown` — Streaming markdown renderer for terminal UIs with checkpoint-based freezing and syntect highlighting. Rendering internals, out of scope for domain 12.
- `xai-grok-markdown-core` — Headless markdown analysis sharing the exact pulldown-cmark option set with the renderer, so analysis matches what is drawn; depends only on pulldown-cmark. Rendering-adjacent, but the shared-parser property is what keeps the two from diverging.
- `xai-grok-mermaid` — Mermaid diagram rendering for the pager, including a PNG cache keyed by source, theme, width and quality tier. Rendering internals, out of scope for domain 12.
- `xai-ratatui-inline` — Custom ratatui widget for printing above a pinned inline region. Rendering internals, out of scope for domain 12.
- `xai-ratatui-textarea` — Forked/extended ratatui textarea widget. Rendering internals, out of scope for domain 12.
- `xai-grok-shared` — Shared utilities used by both xai-grok-shell and its downstream clients; sits upstream of the shell so it must never depend on it. Infrastructure, no capability claim.
- `xai-tty-utils` — TTY-safety process spawning: when a raw-mode TUI owns the controlling TTY, every child must be detached or its grandchildren (npm, git, pinentry, ssh-agent) can open `/dev/tty` and spew escape codes or credential prompts onto the live screen. Infrastructure with a real safety rationale, but no capability of its own.
- `xai-dirs` — Home-directory resolution: USERPROFILE-first `home_dir` plus grok-home (`$GROK_HOME` or `<home>/.grok`), with distinct functions for the cached/created path, the no-cwd-fallback path, and the override-ignoring default. Infrastructure.
- `xai-grok-paths` — Type-safe path wrappers for absolute and relative UTF-8 paths. Infrastructure.
- `xai-file-utils` — Local data collection: upload queueing and S3-compatible blob storage; also the credential holder that implements `xai-grok-auth`'s traits. Infrastructure for ops and credentials.
- `xai-grok-test-support` — Shared test utilities for the grok-build crates. Test infrastructure, no capability claim.
- `build` — `crates/build/xai-proto-build`, the protobuf codegen support crate. Build infrastructure, no runtime capability.
- `codegen` — `crates/codegen` is a directory of 82 crate modules, not a crate. The classifier emitted it as an `unmapped` module; it is the container for every other entry in this table.
- `common` — `crates/common` is a directory, not a crate - the same classifier artifact as `crates/codegen`. It holds 12 crates that the 82-module checklist does not include, and three of them carry mechanisms this source would otherwise appear to lack entirely: `xai-message-delivery-core` (the source-typed operation authorization matrix, the delivery-identity triple check and the turn-bound safe-point lifecycle in `loop`), `xai-interjection-core` (mid-turn interjection buffering and framing in `loop`) and `xai-grok-compaction` (context compaction; read only at the level of its crate boundary here, not inventoried). The remainder are `xai-tool-protocol`, `xai-tool-runtime`, `xai-tool-types`, `xai-tracing`, `xai-circuit-breaker`, `xai-test-utils` and the four `xai-computer-hub-*` crates. `xai-tool-types` supplies `SubagentCapabilityMode`, used by the `subagent` mechanisms above.
- `xai-grok-subagent-resolution` — The pure resolution phase of subagent spawning: per-field precedence (explicit override > role > persona > parent), the most-restrictive-wins capability-mode meet, persona and role prompt loading, and resume identity validation that pins type and persona while silently dropping a model override. Contributes `capability-mode-meet-and-resume-identity-pin` to `subagent`.
- `xai-grok-compaction` — Context compaction, in `crates/common/xai-grok-compaction` rather than in the codegen checklist. Confirmed present and named in the brief as a rich `context` source, but not opened during this pass, so no mechanism entry is claimed for it. Its absence from the domain list is an evidence gap, not a finding that grok lacks compaction - `xai-compaction-transcript` renders what it produces, and that relationship is the reason a follow-up should read this crate next.

### cc-custom（36 模組）

- `context` — Contributes to: priority-command-queue (src/context/QueuedMessageContext.tsx subscribes to the command queue) and swarm-mailbox (src/context/mailbox.tsx bridges the teammate mailbox). The remaining files (notifications, overlayContext, promptOverlayContext, modalContext, stats, fpsMetrics) are React context providers for presentation, which the interface domain scope places out of scope.
- `hooks` — 93 files, mostly React hooks. Contributes to: approval-decision-single-shot and sdk-permission-prompt-channel (hooks/toolPermission/handlers/interactiveHandler.ts, coordinatorHandler.ts, swarmWorkerHandler.ts); swarm-mailbox (useInboxPoller, useMailboxBridge), task-list-coordination (useTasksV2, useTaskListWatcher), in-process-teammate-task and team-roster-and-identity (useSwarmInitialization, useSwarmPermissionPoller); early-stdin-capture and the command-queue drain (useCommandQueue, useQueueProcessor, useCancelRequest, useDeferredHookMessages). The rest are terminal/UI hooks outside the backend interface scope.
- `memdir` — Contributes to: memdir-auto-memory-store (memdir.ts, paths.ts), memory-taxonomy-and-scan-index (memoryTypes.ts, memoryScan.ts), memory-relevance-recall (findRelevantMemories.ts), memory-staleness-annotation (memoryAge.ts) and team-memory-and-secret-guard (teamMemPaths.ts, teamMemPrompts.ts).
- `plugins` — Contributes to plugin-install-and-component-integration: builtinPlugins.ts holds the built-in plugin registry and bundled/index.ts is its empty-bodied initialiser, which is the evidence that the built-in registry is inert.
- `skills` — Contributes to skills-discovery-and-precedence (loadSkillsDir.ts) and bundled-skills-registry (bundledSkills.ts plus the 13 modules under bundled/).
- `constants` — Infrastructure: supplies the numeric limits cited by other mechanisms rather than a capability of its own — toolLimits.ts feeds tool-result-output-spill and per-message-aggregate-result-budget, toolLimits.ts and tools.ts feed tool-pool-assembly-and-exposure-registry, betas.ts feeds beta-header-negotiation, errorIds.ts feeds agent-and-tool-progress-summaries, and product.ts/xml.ts feed system-prompt construction.
- `native-ts` — Contributes no backend mechanism. yoga-layout is the flexbox layout engine consumed by the Ink renderer (layout is out of the interface domain scope), file-index is a pure-TypeScript port of a Rust file-index module consumed only by hooks/fileSuggestions.ts (an input affordance), and color-diff is diff colouring.
- `schemas` — Contributes to hook-execution-engine: schemas/hooks.ts defines the five hook type schemas (command, prompt, http, agent, exec) and the shared `if` condition schema that the hook engine matches against.
- `types` — Infrastructure, but carries three contracts cited by mechanisms: types/permissions.ts holds EXTERNAL_PERMISSION_MODES and INTERNAL_PERMISSION_MODES (permission-mode-model), types/hooks.ts holds the hook input shapes (hook-permission-gate), and types/generated/events_mono/* holds the protobuf telemetry manifest (telemetry-event-manifest-and-sanitisation).
- `upstreamproxy` — Contributes no mechanism: the directory exists but contains zero files.
- `utils` — The largest module and the shared substrate. Contributes to: effect-scoped-abort-and-cleanup and abort-and-interrupt-semantics (abortController.ts), priority-command-queue (messageQueueManager.ts), queue draining (queueProcessor.ts), early-stdin-capture (earlyInput.ts), jsonl-transcript-append-log, parent-uuid-conversation-chain, buffered-transcript-write-pipeline and tombstone-rewrite-guard (sessionStorage.ts), session-pid-registry-and-reaper (concurrentSessions.ts), file-checkpoint-snapshot-and-rewind (fileHistory.ts), context-window-resolution (context.ts), os-sandbox-delegation and sandbox-policy-model (sandbox/sandbox-adapter.ts), the whole permission ladder (permissions/permissions.ts, pathValidation.ts, denialTracking.ts, dangerousPatterns.ts, yoloClassifier.ts, bashClassifier.ts, permissionSetup.ts, bypassPermissionsKillswitch.ts, shadowedRuleDetection.ts), ssrf-guard-for-http-hooks (hooks/ssrfGuard.ts) and hook-execution-engine (hooks.ts), token-estimation (tokens.ts), tool-result spill (toolResultStorage.ts), terminal capability and render seams (fullscreen.ts, renderOptions.ts, exportRenderer.tsx), model discovery and cost (model/*, providers/*, modelCost.ts, effort.ts), the two plan-mode and deferred-tool utilities, and most retrieval and ops mechanisms (claudemd.ts, glob.ts, ripgrep.ts, readFileInRange.ts, fileStateCache.ts, attachments.ts, pdf.ts, docIndexing, listSessionsImpl.ts, sessionStoragePortable.ts, agenticSessionSearch.ts, sessionTitle.ts, codeIndexing.ts, sessionUrl.ts, memoryFileDetection.ts, diagLogs.ts, log.ts, errorLogSink.ts, gracefulShutdown.ts, profilerBase.ts, queryProfiler.ts, heapDumpService.ts, fpsTracker.ts, telemetry/instrumentation.ts, autoUpdater.ts, localInstaller.ts, doctorDiagnostic.ts, doctorContextWarnings.ts).
- `assistant` — Contributes no mechanism: the directory exists but contains zero files.
- `buddy` — Contributes no mechanism. It is a gated presentation feature (a companion sprite and its notification); every entry point is wrapped in feature('BUDDY') and prompt.ts gates its prompt attachment on the same flag, so the module is inert in this build.
- `components` — 367 files, almost all React components outside the interface domain scope. Three exceptions are cited: SandboxPromptFooterHint.tsx and SandboxViolationExpandedView.tsx contribute to sandbox-violation-surfacing, SentryErrorBoundary.ts contributes to graceful-shutdown-and-crash-containment, and Stats.tsx contributes to local-usage-analytics-and-insights.
- `ink` — 100 files; the renderer internals (layout, wrap, styles, reconciler, dom) are out of scope. Contributes to: terminal-capability-detection and the OSC 9;4/DEC 2026 predicates (terminal.ts), terminal-query-protocol (terminal-querier.ts, termio/*), hyperlink-capability-detection (supports-hyperlinks.ts) and screen-mode-policy (components/AlternateScreen.tsx).
- `keybindings` — Contributes no backend mechanism. It is a client-side keymap resolver (parser, match, resolver, reserved shortcuts, user bindings) that maps keystrokes to in-process command ids; it produces no event or stream contract a backend consumes.
- `proactive` — Contributes to proactive-self-tick-loop (loop domain) and gated-presentation-io-modules (interface domain): index.ts is the tick state machine and useProactive.ts schedules the synthetic tick prompt, both inert behind feature('PROACTIVE').
- `screens` — Contributes to gated-presentation-io-modules (REPL.tsx is where the presentation layer meets the engine), doctor-and-context-diagnostics (Doctor.tsx renders installation type and context warnings) and feedback-channels (REPL.tsx is where the feedback entry points were removed).
- `vim` — Contributes no backend mechanism: motions, operators, text objects and transitions are a client-side modal-editing state machine for the text input.
- `voice` — Contributes no mechanism: the directory exists but contains zero files.
- `coordinator` — Contributes to gated-delegation-modes (subagent domain): coordinatorMode.ts holds isCoordinatorMode() and the coordinator system prompt, both unreachable because the gate is a false feature flag.
- `query` — The loop itself. Contributes to sequential-effect-state-machine, turn-budget-ladder, effect-scoped-abort-and-cleanup, abort-and-interrupt-semantics, mid-turn-command-drain-steering, stop-hook-continuation-loop (runtime/, contracts/, policies/), max-output-token-recovery-and-escalation, token-budget-continuation, reactive-compact-on-overflow, context-collapse-and-history-snip, cached-microcompact, parallel-tool-batching-and-commit-ordering and tool-hook-lifecycle (effects/), and supplies the production entry point at src/query.ts.
- `bootstrap` — Contributes to session identity and process bootstrap rather than to the service domain: bootstrap/state.ts is the module-level session state (session id, cwd, session switching, MCP clients), and bootstrapMacro.ts injects the MACRO globals consumed by ops install-and-update and feedback-channels.
- `cli` — Contributes to structured-io-transport, ndjson-line-safety, sdk-permission-prompt-channel, sdk-output-format-exit-and-error-surface, user-message-replay-and-dedup, print-mode-streaming-loop (cli/print.ts, cli/structuredIO.ts, cli/ndjsonSafeStringify.ts, cli/exit.ts), mcp-server-mode-over-stdio (handlers/mcp.tsx) and in-process-sdk-mcp-bridge.
- `server` — Contributes to direct-connect-http-ws-client (the client half only; the server modules it imports do not exist in this tree) and to sdk-permission-prompt-channel (server/permissionMessages.ts formats the outbound permission request text).
- `services` — The most cross-cutting module: it spans eight domains. Contributes to context (compact/*, tokenEstimation.ts, SessionMemory/*, extractMemories/*, autoDream/*, MagicDocs/*), tools (tools/*), model (api/*, subscriptions/*), extension (mcp/*, lsp/*, plugins/*), retrieval (SessionMemory, teamMemorySync, AgentSummary), ops (analytics/*, diagnosticTracking.ts, notifier.ts, internalLogging.ts, tokenEstimation.ts), loop and subagent (toolUseSummary, PromptSuggestion/speculation.ts, AgentSummary) and the ops/context VCR harness (vcr.ts).
- `ssh` — Contributes to ssh-remote-ndjson-transport: createSSHSession.ts carries the remote command construction and SSHSessionManager.ts the NDJSON transport over the ssh child, both inert behind feature('SSH_REMOTE').
- `migrations` — Contributes to config-migration-ladder: the seven migration modules are the idempotent settings rewrites driven by main.tsx runMigrations().
- `state` — Contributes to todo-durable-state (AppState.todos is the durable checklist), mcp-connection-lifecycle-and-recovery (state holds connection status) and subagent task state: store.ts is a minimal subscribe/setState store with Object.is equality and an onChange hook, AppState.tsx the typed state shape and selectors.ts the derived reads.
- `jobs` — Contributes no mechanism: the directory exists but contains zero files.
- `tasks` — Contributes to subagent-task-state-machine and agent-task-cancellation (LocalAgentTask), background-agent-parent-wake (LocalAgentTask notification path), in-process-teammate-task (InProcessTeammateTask), gated-task-type-stubs (LocalWorkflowTask, MonitorMcpTask, DreamTask) and shell-process-control-and-disk-spill (utils/task/diskOutput.ts is cited; LocalShellTask owns the background shell task lifecycle).
- `tools` — Contributes to the entire tools domain (registry, validation, orchestration, spill, shell and PowerShell security, file edit and read limits, sleep guard), plus subagent delegation (AgentTool/*, Team*, SendMessage, Task*), extension (ToolSearchTool, WorkflowTool, ScheduleCronTool, TodoWriteTool, Enter/ExitPlanMode), retrieval (FileReadTool, GlobTool, GrepTool, ListMcpResourcesTool, ReadMcpResourceTool) and safety (BashTool/shouldUseSandbox.ts, bashPermissions.ts, bashSecurity.ts, PowerShellTool/*).
- `commands` — Contributes to extension, ops and service: commands.ts is the slash-command registry that imports all 160 files; workflow-engine (workflow stubs), feedback-channels (the fifteen isEnabled:()=>false stub commands), doctor-and-context-diagnostics, cost-and-token-accounting (/cost), local-usage-analytics-and-insights (/stats, /insights), plan-mode-and-plan-files indicators and the mcp/plugin/skills/hooks management commands.
- `entrypoints` — Contributes to the service domain (sdk/coreSchemas.ts, controlSchemas.ts, coreTypes.generated.ts, agentSdkTypes.ts, mcp.ts, init.ts), to safety (sandboxTypes.ts is the sandbox policy schema) and to interface (HOOK_EVENTS in coreSchemas.ts is the shared event manifest; cli.tsx is an argv surface).
- `moreright` — Contributes no mechanism: useMoreRight.tsx is a one-file stub for external builds that returns pass-through callbacks and render: () => null.
- `outputStyles` — Contributes no standalone mechanism: loadOutputStylesDir.ts loads markdown output styles from .claude/output-styles directories through the same markdown-config loader the skills family uses; the result only parameterises the system prompt, so no distinct capability row was warranted.

## 附錄 B — 分類器修正與空域（149 筆修正）

Phase 0 的域提示是關鍵字分類器給的，**不是權威**。以下列出各源代理實際更正的部分；這些修正本身就是「為什麼機器分類不足以做這件事」的證據。

| 源 | 模組 | 分類器提示 | 實際歸屬 | 理由 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IH | `guard-timeout` | infra | safety | The classifier read 'timeout' as tooling. The package is a monotonic cascade guard on tools/execute registered through the plugin kernel: it enforces a tool's declared timeoutMs and substitutes a structured TOOL_TIMEOUT, distinguishes a parent cancel from its own timer by a local flag, and is the OUTER partner guard-retry depends on for mount order. That is a tool-execution safety mechanism, not build/test infrastructure. |
| IH | `settings` | interface | service | The hint came from 'settings = UI'. The package is a backend durable-store surface: layered raw-document merge with a master-source write rule, a section leaf-patch protocol with per-section revisions and conflict codes, a polling hot-reload watcher gated on a normalized-view change, comment-preserving JSON patching, and no migration chain. web-host maps its error codes to HTTP 400/409; the persistence-chain reads (not writes) resolve the sandbox mode and the model catalog. Only its consumer is a UI. |
| IH | `compaction` | context | context | Hint is correct for the domain, but the entry that matters is inverted from the classifier's assumption: the engine exists in full (six mechanisms) and is reachable from NO shipped host, because createSessionService and createSessionAssembly both drop `compact` when it is undefined and no production caller supplies it, and createAgent's compact() then returns {compacted:false}. Recorded as an explicit unwired-status mechanism rather than as a live context feature. |
| IH | `workspace` | tools | tools | Hint is correct that the module is touched by the tool/route plane, but it ships no model-facing tool: it is a durable registry document plus a bounded file walker used as the '@' file-reference provider, and it performs NO filesystem mutation and no path realpath/stat itself (create() does pure string normalization; the host route owns the trust boundary). It belongs with the durable-store surfaces (service/session), not with the tool families. |
| IH | `todo` | tools | subagent | Reassigned because the taxonomy's own scope text puts 'todos and goals as durable extension state' in `extension`/planning and the seed list pairs todo with goal/plan-mode. Mechanically it is a tool (todo_write) whose state is session-log events with a pure projection, so it participates in the delegated-work/planning state family rather than in tool execution; it executes nothing and has no guard/approval surface. |
| IH | `skills` | extension | extension | Domain hint stands, but the module is also a RETRIEVAL mechanism: bodies stay deferred (list/search touch summaries only), the searchable text is name+description with no schema, and the shadow selector exists to gather offline evidence about what a variant selector would pick. Its retrieval half is recorded under `extension` alongside tool-search rather than under `retrieval`, which the taxonomy reserves for session search, memory and attachments. |
| IH | `tui` | interface | interface | Hint is right only if the interface domain is read as the BACKEND interface. Most of this 193-file package is rendering, layout, theming and input handling, which the taxonomy explicitly excludes; the in-scope parts are the embedded backend bridge (session-log subscription -> renderer events, 16 ms batching, seq cursor), the hard slash-capability gate, and the screen-mode relaunch seam. |
| IH | `tui-core` | interface | infra | The package is terminal/engine primitives (input decoding, frame engine, capability probe) consumed BY the TUI. It contributes no backend-interface contract of its own — the event vocabulary, batching and capability gate all live in packages/tui — so it is infrastructure for the interface rather than a mechanism in it. |
| IH | `sandbox` | safety | safety | Domain stands; the classifier's 'safety' bucket hides that a large part of this package is INERT. The whole escalation ladder (WIDER_MODES, ESCALATION_TARGETS, approveEscalation, the denial/hint markers, validateEscalationArgs) and writableRoots/canonicalPath have no production caller, and no tool schema declares the sandbox_permissions/justification arguments the marker text advertises. Recorded with explicit unwired status. |
| dsh | `api/workspace-controller` | tools | service | Both are Typert @Remote facades over the workspace/fs services exposed to the browser; the tools live in packages/fs and packages/workspace. |
| dsh | `api/workspace-files` | tools | service | Both are Typert @Remote facades over the workspace/fs services exposed to the browser; the tools live in packages/fs and packages/workspace. |
| dsh | `client/connection` | service | interface | These client plugins do carry a real engine seam (stream reconnection, host command catalog merge, sessionFeedback Remote, open-in-app Remote, user-questions and approval waterfall answerers) and are recorded under interface/safety. |
| dsh | `client/file-upload` | service | interface | The client half of the attachment upload path (HTTP route, upload protocol, contract types). Client-side transport plumbing; the committed content is handled by attachment/attachment. |
| dsh | `client/hmr` | service | infra | Client-side hot-module-reload receiver for client-plugin bundles, plus its event/invariant plumbing. Development tooling. |
| dsh | `client/locale` | service | interface | Client i18n catalogue and locale resolution. Presentation only. |
| dsh | `client/modules` | service | interface | The client plugin system (manifest parsing, module resolution, lifecycle wiring; src/client/index.ts ~1036 lines). It composes browser plugins and contributes no engine capability. |
| dsh | `client/resources` | service | interface | Client resource-provider registry (how a client plugin contributes a resource such as a file preview). Composition plumbing. |
| dsh | `client/store` | service | interface | Client-side reactive store contract and implementation. Presentation state only. |
| dsh | `client/ui-approval` | safety | interface | These client plugins do carry a real engine seam (stream reconnection, host command catalog merge, sessionFeedback Remote, open-in-app Remote, user-questions and approval waterfall answerers) and are recorded under interface/safety. |
