| codex | `agent-graph-store` |  |  | It stores parent/child thread-spawn edges with open/closed status and BFS descendant listing — a delegation-topology store, not a loop component (agent-graph-store/src/store.rs:13-59). |
| codex | `agent-identity` |  |  | It is the cryptographic service identity for ChatGPT-hosted agent tasks (Ed25519 key material, signed task registration, JWKS-verified JWT, task-id decryption) plus the AgentPath identity model — not a loop component (agent-identity/src/lib.rs:38-40, :247-315). |
| codex | `external-agent-migration` |  |  | It imports configuration and memory state from other agents into a Codex session; the capability is migration/portability, gated by Feature::ExternalAgentMigration (Stage::Removed) and Feature::ExternalAgentMemoryImport (UnderDevelopment, OFF) (features/src/lib.rs:1288-1293, :990-995). |
| codex | `cloud-tasks / cloud-tasks-client / cloud-tasks-mock-client` |  |  | These are a client for the hosted cloud-task backend (create/list/apply a task), not a sub-agent spawn/control protocol. Mapping them to the subagent domain would falsely credit Codex with a delegation mechanism it does not have here. |
| codex | `code-mode / code-mode-host / code-mode-protocol / code-mode-runtime` |  |  | Code mode also drives the tools domain: `Feature::CodeModeOnly` restricts model-visible tools to the code-mode entrypoints and `ToolExposure::CodeModeOnly` exists in the exposure ladder (features/src/lib.rs:619-620, core/src/tools/spec_plan.rs:241). Noted so the tools row is not scored as if code mode were purely an extension. |
| codex | `collaboration-mode-templates` |  |  | The templates are not consumed as extension code; they are rendered into the Plan collaboration mask's developer_instructions and injected as a role=developer world-state section, so they are part of the context-assembly mechanism (core/src/context/world_state/collaboration_mode.rs:122-143). Feature::CollaborationModes is itself Stage::Removed with default_enabled true (features/src/lib.rs:1432-1437). |
| codex | `connectors` |  |  | The connector/app policy evaluator is invoked from the tool orchestrator (core/src/tools/orchestrator.rs:69 imports AppToolPolicyEvaluator), so connector policy is part of tool-call admission, not only an extension catalog. |
| codex | `config` |  |  | The same crate carries the permission-profile catalog and requirement checks (safety) and the provider/credential configuration types (model), not just operator-facing settings (core/src/config/permission_profile_catalog.rs:20-140). |
| codex | `cloud-config` |  |  | Listed for completeness; its 14-line lib is a re-export surface for cloud-sourced config entries (cloud-config/src/lib.rs:1-14). |
| codex | `keyring-store` |  |  | It is a credential-storage backend for auth persistence (the OS keyring behind Feature::SecretAuthStorage), not a sandbox or permission mechanism (core/src/config/auth_keyring.rs:110-118, features/src/lib.rs:846-851). |
| codex | `secrets` |  |  | It is the encrypted credential store, not a confinement mechanism; it also supplies the secret redaction applied before rollout transcripts are handed to memory extraction. |
| codex | `process-hardening` |  |  | The mechanism is disabling core dumps, clearing ptrace attach and stripping LD_*/DYLD_* before main(), with dedicated non-zero exit codes — confinement of the agent process itself (process-hardening/src/lib.rs:1-60). |
| codex | `execpolicy` |  |  | The decision/rule model is the safety policy layer for command execution; the registry-based domain classifier put it in tools and did not record the safety half. |
| codex | `shell-escalation` |  |  | It implements the escalated-permission rerun protocol, which is the second rung of the approval/escalation ladder, not merely a shell utility (shell-escalation/src/lib.rs:1-39). |
| codex | `bwrap` |  |  | Bubblewrap is the Linux filesystem-isolation backend; the crate is a thin re-export shim over linux-sandbox/src/bwrap.rs and vendor/bubblewrap. |
| codex | `linux-sandbox / windows-sandbox-rs / sandboxing` |  |  | Recorded as a confirmation rather than a correction: all three are genuine confinement backends, and windows-sandbox-rs in particular carries the WFP network filters and the elevated-vs-restricted-token split. |
| codex | `history` |  |  | It holds the annotated history container that both persistence and compaction operate on; it is the substrate for the compaction/world-state projection, so it also contributes to context. |
| codex | `message-history` |  |  | It is `~/.codex/history.jsonl`, a global append-only user-message log independent of any session's rollout; keeping it under session risks being read as per-session persistence. |
| codex | `rollout-trace` |  |  | It is a diagnostics/trace-bundle format with a writer and a reducer, distinct from the durable session log; it belongs with observability rather than session persistence. |
| codex | `app-server-protocol-noop-macros` |  |  | It supplies no-op macro stubs so protocol types compile without the experimental-api derive; recorded so it is not counted as a mechanism. |
| codex | `features` |  |  | The FEATURES table is the authority on which capabilities are on by default and which are Stage::Removed; its only ops output is the feature-state counter (features/src/lib.rs:490-505). |
| codex | `workload-identity` |  |  | It supplies workload-identity credentials for hosted deployments; the MCP server refuses to start when it is selected, which is a credential decision, not a delegation mechanism (mcp-server/src/lib.rs:211-219). |

| grok | `xai-acp-lib` | extension | service | This crate *is* the Agent Client Protocol transport - agent/client channel pair, gateway sender/receiver, message boxing, line-buffered and stdin readers, normalization (xai-acp-lib/src/lib.rs:1-30). The taxonomy places ACP under `service` (domain 9 scope: 'Transport surface for external consumers: ... ACP') and the `extension` domain is for MCP/LSP/skills/hooks/plugins. The keyword classifier matched the word 'acp' against extension surfaces rather than reading the scope. |
| grok | `xai-workflow` | subagent | extension | The engine/journal/host modules of a workflow engine. The taxonomy names `xai-workflow` explicitly among the `extension` seed modules ('workflow engines'), because a workflow engine is an extension surface even though a workflow run fans out to agents. Its 8 files are engine, host, journal, meta, run, validate and a name-length cap - there is no delegation or task-state machinery in it. |

### 各源明確的空域（12）

| 源 | 域 | 原因 |
|---|---|---|
| IH | `retrieval` | Thin rather than absent, and what exists is not memory: the only two retrieval mechanisms are session search/lineage over a disposable sqlite FTS index and the image-attachment store plus read_image (three with session listing/title derivation). There is NO cross-session memory, no agent-written memory store, no codebase graph and no embedding/vector index anywhere in the 69 modules — the string 'memory' appears only as a design-note parity reference, and the file-reference provider (workspace's bounded walker) is a picker surface rather than a retrieval index. The domain is recorded with three entries instead of padding. |
| IH | `ops` | Recorded but shallow by design: telemetry, feedback and the settings document are the whole surface. There is NO crash handler / crash-report pipeline, no update or distribution mechanism, no benchmarking harness and no cost accounting beyond token estimation — cost is expressed only as token counts in telemetry rows and the budget ladder. GET /api/telemetry is deliberately absent from the web host, so the emitted stream has no in-repo consumer either. |
| codex | `interface (as a rendering domain)` |  |
| codex | `service (ACP)` |  |
| codex | `extension (LSP)` |  |
| codex | `retrieval (full-text index)` |  |
| codex | `subagent (durable task state machine with parent wake on a job object)` |  |
| codex | `ops (benchmarking harness)` |  |
| codex | `ops (crash telemetry beyond terminal restore)` |  |
| opencode | `safety` | No OS-level confinement exists in this source. A repo-wide search of opencode 1.18.30 TS sources for bwrap\|seatbelt\|sandbox-exec\|landlock\|libseccomp\|nsjail\|firejail returns zero matches. safety therefore contains only a permission model (ctx.ask in sensitive tools) with the shell spawned directly - there is no sandbox backend, no read/write isolation and no escalation ladder to record. The absence is the finding; it is recorded here rather than as a padded mechanism. |
| ocode-fork | `safety` | Same absence in the fork. No bwrap\|seatbelt\|sandbox-exec\|landlock\|libseccomp\|nsjail\|firejail matches anywhere in its TS sources; the only "sandbox" identifiers are the project sandboxes path column, a path-normalisation migration of that column, and codemode JS-level types - none confine a spawned process. The fork keeps the V2 permission engine as its only gate. |
| grok | `retrieval` | Not absent - deliberately deferred rather than claimed hollow. The three retrieval crates are real and were read: `xai-grok-session-search` (FTS5 cache with BM25, quarantine-and-recreate recovery, one-process reindex lease), `xai-codebase-graph` (tree-sitter go-to-definition and go-to-references over a full repo index) and `xai-fuzzy-file-search` (ignore-walk plus nucleo matcher on a background thread, degrading per stage rather than aborting). Evidence for all three is in `moduleCoverage`, but a second research pass over their internals (schema, lease mechanics, ranking, reindex triggers) had not returned when this file was written, so no mechanism entries with verified line citations were emitted. Reading these three crates fully is the highest-value next step for this source. |

## 附錄 D — opencode fork 差異（15 項）

| 領域 | 變更 | 出處 |
|---|---|---|
| extension/commands | CoreV2 source-composed command registry: upstream composes commands imperatively inside packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts, whereas the fork exposes a CommandV2 that plugins contribute to through a Source list, with a beforeExecute hook that rewrites command parts before expansion. | `packages/core/src/command.ts:18`、`packages/core/src/command.ts:68`、`packages/core/src/command.ts:79`、`packages/core/src/command.ts:100`、`packages/core/src/session/prompt-expansion.ts:307`、`packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts:105`、`packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts:134` |
| extension/commands | INIT and REVIEW are exported named constants in the fork (CommandV2.INIT / CommandV2.REVIEW) and consumed by a registered plugin; upstream keeps them as Default.INIT / Default.REVIEW strings private to the command service. | `packages/core/src/command.ts:15`、`packages/core/src/command.ts:16`、`packages/core/src/plugin/command.ts:15`、`packages/core/src/plugin/command.ts:19`、`packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts:46`、`packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts:70`、`packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts:79` |
| extension/mcp | MCP prompts self-register as a command Source in the fork, so a prompt can resolve its template lazily through the source interface; upstream iterates mcp.prompts() inline while building the command map. | `packages/core/src/mcp/runtime.ts:85`、`packages/core/src/plugin/command.ts:6`、`packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts:105` |
| extension/skills | The skill frontmatter slash opt-out is honoured in the fork: skills are filtered on slash !== false in both get and list. Upstream parses and stores the same field but never consults it, so a skill marked slash:false is still exposed as a slash command upstream. | `packages/core/src/skill.ts:57`、`packages/core/src/skill.ts:119`、`packages/core/src/command.ts:85`、`packages/core/src/command.ts:97`、`packages/opencode/src/command/index.ts:134` |
| extension/prompt-expansion | Command execution is extracted into a SessionPromptExpansion service (331 lines) that owns argument expansion and agent guidance; upstream performs the same expansion inside the legacy command/prompt path. | `packages/core/src/session/prompt-expansion.ts:71`、`packages/core/src/session/prompt-expansion.ts:82`、`packages/core/src/session/prompt-expansion.ts:307`、`packages/core/src/session/prompt-expansion.ts:326` |
| loop/engines | Two session engines coexist - classic and kernel. Each session fixes its engine at creation; the router reads the stored engine and returns the matching implementation, and a missing kernel registration raises KernelUnavailableError without ever falling back to classic. The kernel is a distinct durable implementation (coordinator, lifecycle store, checkpoints, recovery planner/executor, tool scheduler) where upstream has only the classic local path. | `packages/core/src/session/execution/router.ts:13`、`packages/core/src/session/execution/router.ts:18`、`packages/core/src/session/execution/router.ts:10`、`packages/core/src/session/execution.ts:54`、`packages/core/src/session/execution.ts:59`、`packages/core/src/session/execution.ts:70`、`packages/core/src/session/kernel/index.ts:19`、`packages/core/src/session/kernel/index.ts:35`、`packages/core/src/session/kernel/tool-scheduler.ts:42`、`packages/core/src/session/execution/local.ts:1` |
| subagent/durability | Durable task submission protocol with a task_notification_outbox table as the durable source of truth; the process-local notification is described in-source as an advisory wakeup only. | `packages/core/src/session/task-notification.ts:10`、`packages/core/src/session/task-notification.ts:31`、`packages/core/src/session/task-submission.ts:18`、`packages/core/src/session/task-submission.ts:411` |
| safety/permissions | Permission V1 to V2 is migrated in place by rewriting stored session permission rows from {permission,pattern,action} to {action,resource,effect} so the column type can switch to PermissionV2.Ruleset. | `packages/core/src/database/migration/20260811000000_session_permission_v2.ts:4`、`packages/core/src/database/migration/20260811000000_session_permission_v2.ts:17`、`packages/core/src/database/migration/20260811000000_session_permission_v2.ts:18`、`packages/core/src/database/migration/20260811000000_session_permission_v2.ts:19` |
| extension/plugin-compat | A v1 plugin compatibility bridge adapts the legacy hook surface (chat.message, chat.params, chat.headers, permission.ask, command.execute.before, tool.execute.before/after, shell.env, auth) onto the V2 plugin runtime. | `packages/core/src/plugin/v1-compat.ts:54`、`packages/core/src/plugin/v1-compat.ts:57`、`packages/core/src/plugin/v1-compat.ts:62`、`packages/core/src/plugin/v1-compat.ts:65`、`packages/core/src/plugin/v1-compat.ts:66`、`packages/core/src/plugin/v1-compat.ts:69` |
| extension/plugin-compat | v1-projection.ts projects V2 model/credential/provider data back into the V1 plugin-facing shapes, including auth projection and provider source labelling. | `packages/core/src/plugin/v1-projection.ts:15`、`packages/core/src/plugin/v1-projection.ts:159`、`packages/core/src/plugin/v1-projection.ts:228`、`packages/core/src/plugin/v1-projection.ts:246` |
| backend/relayering | The backend engine was relayered into packages/core: the fork has 650 ts files under packages/core versus 479 upstream, and subsystems that upstream keeps in packages/opencode/src moved into core - command, mcp (core/src/mcp/runtime.ts is 1102 lines), lsp, skill and the plugin runtime. Extension modules that exist upstream at packages/opencode/src/<x> may therefore be absent or reduced in the fork. | `packages/core/src/command.ts:1`、`packages/core/src/mcp/runtime.ts:1`、`packages/core/src/plugin/runtime.ts:1`、`packages/core/src/skill.ts:1` |
| backend/compaction | Compaction moved into core as packages/core/src/session/compaction.ts, and the kernel schedules a fenced compaction phase under the session lease; upstream keeps compaction in packages/opencode/src/session/compaction.ts. | `packages/core/src/session/compaction.ts:1`、`packages/core/src/session/kernel/index.ts:22`、`packages/opencode/src/session/compaction.ts:1` |
| session/migrations | The fork retires the legacy transcript: a session-message tombstone migration is followed by a migration that drops the legacy transcript tables. | tools/exposure | The fork adds durable tool-discovery state, gating tool exposure through the kernel tool catalog with a persisted discovery table; upstream materialises the tool set per session without durable discovery state. | `packages/core/src/session/tool-discovery.ts:1`、`packages/core/src/database/migration/20260815080734_session-tool-discovery.ts:1`、`packages/core/src/tool/catalog.ts:1` |
| safety/environment | Neither source ships an OS-level sandbox, so the fork does not close the upstream gap: both expose the host user authority through a permission gate only. Recorded as a non-delta so the two sources are not mistakenly read as differing on confinement. | `packages/core/src/tool/bash.ts:340`、`packages/core/src/project/sql.ts:16`、`packages/opencode/src/tool/shell.ts:303`、`packages/opencode/src/permission/index.ts:216` |
